# Autonomy Standing Order (SMH programme) — Knowledge pointer note

Provenance: [onboarding interview](metadata://onboarding/interview), supplied by Andrea 19 Nov 2026, saved and corrected the same session (session 126) against the programme bundle's own signed documents.

This note is a Knowledge-side pointer. The bundle documents are authoritative on any conflict:

- pipeline/docs/standing_order_autonomy.md — SIGNED 2026-11-19 at the directorate sitting (his "Sign as drafted"). From that date the generative audit front F6 is ACTIVE, proposal-form re-ranking is lawful, and the directorate readout is standing cadence.
- pipeline/docs/smh_frontier_v0.md — the single direction-selection work list (fronts F1-F8 with entry criteria, what each feeds, and its own death condition; the read-frontier-first session contract; the absolute gate list; the append-only frontier log and re-ranking proposals).
- pipeline/docs/decision_queue_v0.md — the owner's pending decision stack.
- pipeline/docs/autonomous_queue_v0.md — the charter-era work list and its classes under autonomy_charter_v1.md.

## Director's cadence

Daily. Asked directly on 19 Nov 2026 (question prompt) which day the directorate sits, his answer was "Daily". This refines the signed order's weekly-directorate line: the readout cadence is daily from 2026-11-19, riding whichever session runs each day. No new scheduled task was created; the daily charter task already fires daily.

## The structure, as signed

- No persistent self between sessions: autonomy lives in the files every session reads on startup.
- Task execution is solved (scheduled tasks); task selection is delegated via the frontier file (one session, one claimed front, outcome appended, new fronts registered); goal re-ranking is proposal-form only, enacted solely by his word.
- Owner-forever gate either way: no arming, ratifying, kill-line moves, sealed-window reads, claim closing, or trading without his dated word. Re-tuning anything post-null is never delegated.
- Note: the fronts his pasted message names (deep-read queue, misses-curriculum base rates, estimator-discipline family, C8 macro-synthesis skeleton, macro timeline backfill) belong to the parallel lineage's programme, not this record's frozen fronts; the two lineages' conventions are never mixed.

## Unchanged

The default assumption is that there is no edge, and the burden of proof is on every signal. SMH is earned by the falsification record, not declared.

# AVFI-NQ Weekly Strategy and Research Programme (v1.0)

Base starting point: Andrea's AVFI v3 vs v2 spread indicator (Pine v5, reference copy in the quant project repo, strategies/avfi_v3_reference.pine). This document synthesises the accumulated 20-book knowledge into a single strategy framework, a pre-registered hypothesis registry, and a validation pipeline. It is the governing document for the strategy phase.

## 0. Standing constraints (non-negotiable)

- Instrument and timeframe: NQ (E-mini Nasdaq-100 futures), weekly bars. This is the established practice and stays.
- Paper trading only until the full pipeline has passed. Simulated profitability is never treated as proof of a real edge (standing directive, echoed by every book in the quant canon).
- Time budget: 10-20 h/week total for trading research plus websites, evenings and part of weekends, never at medicine's expense. The cadence in section 7 is designed around this.
- Andrea retains final authority on all decisions. The programme's job is to make the decisions knowable, not to take them.

## 1. Layer synthesis: what each book contributes

The 20 books are not a flat library; they stack into six layers, each with a defined role in this strategy:

- Structure layer (Balan, Elliott Wave Principle): the weekly wave count provides positional context only. It sets the prior (trend phase, maturity, terminal risk, invalidation levels). It never provides the entry trigger. Its subjectivity is the reason it is confined to this role and its confluence value is tested as an exploratory hypothesis (H3).
- Flow layer (Williams/VSA; Hasbrouck; Bouchaud et al.): AVFI is the formalised effort-vs-result measure. Williams' verbal rules (no demand, no supply, effort vs results, climax) map directly onto the indicator's gate, dead zone, and volume-surprise terms. Hasbrouck's efficient-price framework gives the interpretation: weekly volume-weighted signed moves are a coarse order-flow imbalance proxy, and persistent imbalance is the only mechanism that produces drift at this frequency.
- Volatility and regime layer (Tsay; Levy; Härdle et al.): the Parkinson range estimator inside AVFI comes straight from Tsay's range-vol section. GARCH persistence means vol states cluster; Student-t conditional tails (nu around 8 on equity-index data) govern sizing; CUSUM/SPC logic guards the z-score against structural breaks.
- Execution layer (Cartea/Jaimungal/Penalva; Johnson): weekly decisions, so the execution problem is small but not zero. Limit orders near reference levels, implementation-shortfall accounting relative to the weekly close decision price, one-tick slippage baseline, commission model. Cartea's regime-dependent logic also licenses the state machine's asymmetric rules.
- Sizing and risk layer (Elliott-Kopp; The Quants; Bartholomew-Biggs): risk measured on CVaR/deviation logic, never Gaussian VaR. Kelly used only as a ceiling reference with a large haircut. Fixed-fractional risk with vol-scaled stops. The optimisation book's flat-optimum lesson governs parameter policy: accept broad plateaus, reject needle-sharp optima.
- Validation and epistemics layer (The Quants; Härdle; Tsay; Reid-Szendrői; the microstructure canon of Harris, Stoll, Madhavan, Foucault, Biais-Glosten-Spatt, Patterson x2): pre-registration before testing, out-of-sample discipline, multiple-testing control, invariance audit of every signal, and standing humility about what weekly OHLCV can reveal. The Patterson books supply the failure modes (1987, August 2007, over-fitted quants); the microstructure canon supplies the honest limits of coarse bars.

## 2. What AVFI v3 actually measures, term by term

Decomposition with book anchors. All of this is definitional, not hypothesis.

- sigmaR = sqrt(pi/2) * EWM(|r|): a mean-absolute-deviation volatility estimator, unbiased for Gaussian returns (E|r| = sqrt(2/pi) sigma). Halflife 30 bars.
- sigmaPk = sqrt(EWM(log(H/L)^2) / (4 ln 2)): the Parkinson estimator; for GBM, E[(ln H/L)^2] = 4 ln 2 sigma^2. Same halflife. (Tsay, range-vol section.)
- sigma3 = max(sigmaR, sigmaPk) per bar: a range-aware volatility floor. The blend avoids underestimating vol on weeks that close quiet but traded wide intraweek.
- z = r / sigma3: the standardised weekly return. The significance gate w(z) = tanh(k(|z| - a)) * sign(z) with dead zone a = 0.2 sigma and slope k = 1: moves below the dead zone contribute nothing (the formal "no demand / no supply" filter), large moves saturate at +/-1 (a climax week cannot run away with the indicator).
- Volume baseline: lv = log(volume), EWM in log space, va = exp(EWM): the geometric average volume. rho = vol / va is the volume surprise ratio relative to its own running geometric mean. m = rho / (1 + rho/gamma) is a Michaelis-Menten-style soft cap (gamma = 2.5): volume spikes compress, so a climax week contributes bounded weight.
- dv = w * m * va; flow = EWM(dv, 65); avfi3 = EMA(flow / va, 3): the signed effective volume, normalised by the average volume, hence dimensionless.
- spread = avfi3 - EMA(avfi2, 105): the fast flow innovation against the slow v2 baseline (v2 differs only in lacking the Parkinson floor and using arithmetic volume averaging). sp_z = rolling 500-bar z-score of the spread.

Invariance audit (Reid-Szendrői, Erlangen discipline): the indicator is built entirely from ratios (r/sigma, vol/va, flow/va) and log-space averages. It is invariant under rescaling of the price axis, the volume axis, and shifts of the price level. It measures structure, not units. This passes the filter that most conventional indicators (any raw price level, any unscaled volume, any indicator quoted in points) fail. The z-scoring adds a further normalisation, at the cost of a 500-bar window assumption (see limitations).

Net definition: AVFI v3 spread is a scale-invariant, volume-weighted, significance-gated weekly order-flow imbalance proxy, expressed as an innovation against a slow baseline. Whether it predicts anything is exactly what the hypotheses below are for. The indicator is a measurement instrument, not a strategy; nothing in its construction guarantees information content.

## 3. The state machine and its interpretation

The label logic defines four states from two signs (spread, emaV2):

1. Momentum up: spread >= 0, emaV2 >= 0. Flow innovation positive inside a positive flow regime. Working interpretation: sustained net buying pressure.
2. Reversal down: spread < 0, emaV2 >= 0. Flow innovation negative inside a positive regime. First warning of fading participation; per VSA, potential distribution or just a pullback.
3. Momentum down: spread < 0, emaV2 < 0. Sustained net selling pressure.
4. Reversal up: spread >= 0, emaV2 < 0. First sign of accumulation within a downtrend; per VSA, potential accumulation or just a bounce.

These interpretations are hypotheses (H1, H2), not facts. The names are labels, not mechanisms.

## 4. Candidate rule sets (v0, pre-registered, to be validated before any trust)

All thresholds provisional until stage 4 sensitivity analysis. Defaults pre-registered as the script's own default parameters (hlVol 30, hlFlow 65, k 1.0, deadZone 0.2, gamma 2.5, smooth 3, emaLen 105, normWin 500, bandK 1.5). No re-optimisation is permitted on the base case; this is the primary defence against multiple-testing inflation.

- Rule set A (state system, primary): enter long at weekly close when the state becomes momentum up with |sp_z| >= 1, provided the wave count is not in a terminal position against the trade (e.g. a counted fifth wave into resistance). Exit on two consecutive closes in reversal down, or sp_z <= -2, or the structural stop. Symmetric short rules with states reversed, tested but expected weaker given index drift (a long-run upward drift is a structural prior, not an edge claim).
- Rule set B (divergence system, exploratory): price makes a 20-week new extreme while the spread z fades against it, coinciding with a candidate terminal wave in the count. Action: tighten stops / stand aside / take profit on longs. Never a standalone entry.
- Rule set C (controls, mandatory): buy-and-hold NQ; 12-week momentum sign flip (simple trend baseline); random entries with the same exits as rule set A. A signal that cannot beat these controls under identical costs has no edge, whatever its in-sample Sharpe (the compact-null discipline from the geometry book).

Stops and sizing for all rule sets: initial stop at 2-3x ATR(20 weekly), or at the wave-structural invalidation level when the count defines one, whichever is closer. Risk per position: 0.5% of paper equity at the stop (1.0% only after H1/H2 pass and the paper stage). Position size = risk / (stop distance x $20/point); use MNQ fractions when one NQ is oversized. One position maximum.

## 5. Hypothesis registry (pre-registered, November 2026)

Power honesty first: NQ CME history runs from June 1999, roughly 1,300 weekly bars. Four states and conservative event counts (tens to low hundreds of entries per rule over 25 years) mean only large, robust effects are detectable. This constraint shapes everything below: the programme is designed to detect or kill a coarse weekly-timing effect, not to fine-tune parameters.

H1, directional information content. Statement: the spread state at weekly close carries information about the next 1-4 weekly returns beyond the unconditional distribution. Variables: state, sp_z bucket, forward returns. Test: event study with conditional return distributions, stationary block bootstrap for the significance of mean forward returns and hit rates, benchmarked against the unconditional weekly distribution and rule set C controls. Pass: at least one state with an economically meaningful conditional edge (working threshold: conditional mean forward return exceeding unconditional by 0.15x weekly vol after costs, stable across both halves of the sample). Fail: kill rule set A as an edge claim; the indicator remains a descriptive tool.

H2, regime asymmetry. Statement: momentum states exhibit continuation (positive autocorrelation of the spread into next-week returns) while reversal states exhibit mean reversion, so state-conditional exits beat unconditional exits. Test: transition matrix of states plus state-conditional drift/vol estimates; compare rule set A with state-conditional exits vs the same entries with fixed-time exits. Pass: state-conditional exits materially improve risk-adjusted results in-sample and hold out-of-sample.

H3, wave-terminal confluence (exploratory, never sole basis). Statement: AVFI divergence at candidate Elliott wave terminals (Andrea's own weekly counts) raises the probability of a multi-week reversal materially. Data required: Andrea's annotated historical wave counts, collected as an event set with dates and invalidation levels recorded at the time they were counted, not reconstructed with hindsight. Test: conditional reversal frequency at terminals with vs without divergence. Limitation registered: observer bias in the counts is unavoidable; this hypothesis informs trade management, not entry, unless it survives the bias discussion explicitly.

H4, v3 vs v2 construction value. Statement: the Parkinson floor and log-volume baseline (v3) improve signal stability and information content relative to v2's construction. Test: matched event study on both constructions with identical everything else; plus parameter-sensitivity comparison. Pass: v3 dominates on stability with at least equal information content; otherwise prefer the simpler v2 and re-register.

H5, parameter plateau. Statement: any real effect lives on a broad parameter plateau; performance concentrated in narrow parameter regions is curve-fitting. Test: heatmap of the base-case objective over the parameter grid (hlVol x hlFlow x emaLen x normWin at minimum); plateau fraction measured. Pass: the default parameters sit inside a connected region holding, say, 70%+ of the best objective; fail: the "edge" is rejected as overfit regardless of its nominal strength (Bartholomew-Biggs flat-optimum lesson; The Quants as cautionary canon).

H6, vol-regime interaction. Statement: conditional edges differ by volatility state (GARCH-style high/low vol, and crash-regime dummies for 2000-02, 2008-09, 2020, 2022). Test: interaction regressions of state dummies on vol regime dummies; report whether any edge concentrates in a single regime, which would both explain and limit it (Cartea's regime-switching logic; Tsay's Markov-switching framework).

Registration discipline: hypotheses are frozen at testing start. Supersession requires a new registered hypothesis with rationale; nothing is edited silently.

## 6. Validation pipeline (stage 0 to stage 8)

Stage 0, data. Weekly OHLCV for NQ continuous futures, June 1999 to present. Source candidates in order of preference: broker/CME end-of-day exports (cleanest, includes real volume), TradingView chart export, Stooq, Yahoo (NQ=F, known to have gaps; use only as cross-check). Roll policy: last-trade continuous is acceptable at the weekly horizon; the roll splice must be documented. Cash index NDX cannot serve (no volume; AVFI requires volume). Deliverable: a validated CSV plus a data-quality report (missing weeks, roll dates, volume anomalies).

Stage 1, replication. Python/pandas port of AVFI v3 and v2 exactly as specified: EWM with halflife and adjust=False semantics (the Pine was deliberately written to match pandas), the tanh gate, the log-volume baseline, the lag convention ([1] on the baseline EWM), warmup handling. Deliverable: unit tests matching TradingView values on an overlapping window within tolerance (1e-4 on indicator values). No hypothesis work before replication passes.

Stage 2, descriptive event study. State occupancy frequencies, persistence, and conditional forward-return distributions; block bootstrap confidence intervals. H1 and H2 live here.

Stage 3, base-case evaluation. Rule sets A and B with the pre-registered default parameters only, no re-optimisation, against the rule set C controls, costs included (below). In-sample segment only (1999-2013).

Stage 4, sensitivity. Parameter heatmaps, plateau analysis (H5), v3 vs v2 comparison (H4), regime interactions (H6). Validation segment 2014-2020 for all surviving variants; holdout 2021-present opened exactly once, at stage 6, and never for tuning.

Stage 5, cost and execution realism. NQ: $20/point, tick 0.25 points = $5. Baseline costs: 1 tick slippage per side plus commission ($1.50-2.50/side/contract retail). Stress the slippage to 2-3 ticks; a weekly edge that dies at 3 ticks of slippage was never an edge. Execution assumption: weekly close decision, next-open fill as the conservative variant; test close-fill vs next-open sensitivity.

Stage 6, statistical reliability. Report every trial run (count and full grid), apply a max-t/deflated-Sharpe-style adjustment over the family of tested variants, block bootstrap p-values for the final rule set, and the single open of the holdout. The number that matters is the multiple-testing-adjusted one.

Stage 7, paper trading forward test. Minimum 26 weeks. Pre-committed: every Sunday the state, z, wave count and planned action are logged before the week resolves; no retroactive edits. Monthly review of discipline adherence and of realised vs expected behaviour. Kill criteria fixed in advance: systematic divergence between paper results and the backtest's confidence intervals triggers investigation, not rationalisation; two consecutive months of rule breaches pauses the programme.

Stage 8, go/no-go. A decision document: what passed, what failed, at what statistical level, and what (if anything) advances toward live consideration. Live trading is explicitly out of scope for this programme version; a live version would be a new document with its own risk annex.

## 7. Weekly operating cadence (fits inside 10-20 h/week)

- Sunday evening analysis session (60-90 min): refresh the weekly NQ wave count, record the AVFI state and z-score, write the week's brief (state, observations tagged by hypothesis, plan with pre-committed levels and stop). During stage 7 this doubles as the paper-trading decision log.
- Research blocks (two or three evenings, 2-3 h each): pipeline stages. Realistic pace: one stage per fortnight, so stages 0-6 span roughly three to four months alongside the weekly practice.
- Everything else (website work, general market reading) shares the same 10-20 h budget; medicine has absolute priority and the budget flexes downward around exams, never upward against it.

## 8. Honest limitations (read before trusting any output)

- Weekly OHLCV is coarse. AVFI sees flow only through the total weekly volume. The intraday signatures the microstructure canon analyses (queue position, hidden volume, intraday imbalance, effective vs realised spread) are invisible at this frequency. The microstructure books' role here is mostly warning, not signalling.
- Roughly 1,300 bars is a small sample. Most parameter differences will be noise. The programme is built to detect or kill coarse effects, and the expected outcome for several hypotheses is a null; nulls are valuable, they prevent live losses.
- The wave count is subjective. H3 carries observer bias by construction and is confined to trade management unless it survives explicit bias treatment.
- Structural breaks will produce spurious z-score extremes; the 500-bar normalisation window assumes a slowly-varying spread distribution. CUSUM-style monitoring of the spread mean is the standing guard, and any signal generated across a detected break gets flagged.
- Survivorship and regime dependence: NQ has lived through the dot-com collapse, 2008, the 2010 flash-crash era, the 2022 rate cycle, and the 2023-24 AI concentration. H6 exists because an edge averaged across these may be an artefact of one regime.
- Paper trading validates process and expectancy estimates, not psychology or funding stress. Even a fully passed pipeline yields paper-validated expectancy only.

## 9. Change control

This document is the programme's version 1.0. Section 5 hypotheses and section 4 rule sets are frozen once stage 2 begins. All changes thereafter append a dated amendment; nothing is silently rewritten. The next session's first action is stage 0.

## Amendment 1 (11 Nov 2026): registration of H7, rule set T, and the single-open holdout battery

Change control: sections 4 and 5 above remain frozen. This amendment registers
new items only, before any holdout evaluation. The holdout 2021+ has never
been evaluated on any rule, state, or return as of this date.

Context: stages 0-5 complete. Rule set A killed (stage 3: A0 Sharpe -0.115 IS
and 0.420 VAL, both at or below the random-entry controls; A1 structurally
vacuous, zero trades ever, because state transitions coincide with the spread
crossing its EMA where sp_z is near zero). H4, H5, H6 all returned nulls
recorded in the stage log. Stage 6a: the 27-combo searched parameter family
fails a White-style reality check (family best Sharpe 0.58, RC p = 0.11).
The only variant with individually significant demeaned-block-bootstrap
p-values in both windows is the rule set C momentum control (Sharpe 0.798 IS,
p = 0.033; 0.508 VAL, p = 0.053). Two candidates survive on priors, not on
the AVFI signal: the bear-capitulation structure and the trend baseline.

H7, bear-capitulation structure (registered hypothesis). Statement: deep-bear
momentum_down occupancy is followed by above-unconditional multi-week bounces
(in-sample 2008-2010: +0.23 sigma next-week, +2.84% at 4 weeks, bootstrap
p = 0.029, but from one historical episode). This is a regime-scale statement,
not a weekly-timing claim. Pre-committed holdout tests, executable only
inside the battery:
- Sample: all weeks with state = momentum_down in 2021+ (default parameters,
  full-series states).
- Gate: if occupancy is under 10 weeks, H7 is recorded UNTESTABLE and no
  further holdout evaluation of H7 occurs.
- Test 1 (descriptive): mean 4-week forward log return of momentum_down weeks
  versus the unconditional 2021+ mean; stationary block bootstrap B = 2000,
  mean block 13 weeks, seed 42, one-sided.
- Test 2 (rule): long at next open after the state becomes momentum_down;
  exit when the state leaves momentum_down or after 12 weeks, whichever is
  first; costs 0.3625 pts per side; compared against buy-and-hold over the
  same span.
- Pass: descriptive edge >= 0.15 weekly sigma with p < 0.05 AND the rule
  beats buy-and-hold on the same span. Anything else rejects H7.

Rule set T, trend baseline (registered rule set). Rule: long NQ at next open
when the 12-week log return of the weekly close is positive at the prior
weekly close, flat otherwise. Signal exit only, no stops. Costs 0.3625 pts
per side. The lookback stays 12 permanently: it is the control value fixed
before any windowed evaluation, and re-optimisation is forbidden. Pre-committed
holdout test (inside the battery): annualised Sharpe on 2021+ versus the
demeaned stationary-block-bootstrap null; pass p < 0.05; reported alongside
drawdown and exposure versus buy-and-hold. If T passes, it advances to stage 7
(26-week paper forward test) under the weekly decision-log protocol of
section 6, no changes permitted.

Single-open battery protocol: the holdout is opened exactly once, in one
session, executing precisely these pre-committed items and nothing else:
the H7 occupancy gate, H7 tests 1 and 2 (if gated through), the rule set T
holdout test, and the descriptive state occupancy table for 2021+ (context
only, not a test). Family multiplicity: the battery contains two tested
hypotheses; any claim of support must survive a max-t adjustment across both.
No other rule, variant, parameter set, or indicator value may touch holdout
data. Opening requires Andrea's explicit go. If nothing passes, the holdout is
recorded as spent on nulls, and future strategy work relies on forward paper
testing only.

# GitHub Tools Catalog (v1.1)

What this is: the result of Andrea's directive to find useful GitHub projects we may need and do not already know. Every project below was confirmed to exist via web search on 12 Nov 2026 (search-confirmed status per entry; no repository was cloned or run in this sandbox, and package installation remains forbidden here, so nothing has been executed). Usage is split into two modes, per project: (a) REFERENCE PORT, meaning we read the source to port specific algorithms into our stdlib/pandas tooling now; (b) PLATFORM STACK, meaning a candidate dependency for the future order-flow platform (the parked ofx/ORDERFLOW programme) where installs will be possible. Nothing in rule set T changed; no amendment implied; no data touched.

## 1. PDF extraction and OCR (our current bottleneck)

The paper_fetch.py toolkit (research/paper_fetch.py, built and validated this session, 11 PDFs) is stdlib-only by necessity. These projects are the reference layer for what it cannot do:

- pdfplumber (jsvine/pdfplumber). Search-confirmed. Built on pdfminer.six; extracts text plus per-character geometry, rectangles, lines and tables. Reference port value: its table-extraction heuristics and char-level tolerance rules are the best public guide for extracting tabular results (Monte Carlo tables, critical values) from papers. Platform stack: yes, for the article-ingestion tool.
- pdfminer.six (pdfminer.six GitHub org). Search-confirmed. The pure-Python parser underneath pdfplumber; handles encoding, CMaps and operator streams. Reference port value: canonical handling of edge cases (Type0 fonts, inline images) that our extractor will eventually hit. Our ToUnicode CMap and /Differences handling now mirrors its approach.
- PyMuPDF / fitz (maintained under the pymupdf org). Search-confirmed via the pdf-extraction-benchmark comparison. C-based MuPDF bindings; fastest and most accurate of the Python extractors in the 2024 arXiv comparative study (arxiv 2410.09871) and the jsvine-independent benchmarks. License caveat: AGPL, which matters for any future website-facing tool; for private research use it is fine. Platform stack: likely the ingestion default.
- pypdf (py-pdf org). Search-confirmed. Pure-Python merge/split/decrypt; its benchmarks repo (py-pdf/benchmarks) is a useful honesty reference for extraction quality claims. We deliberately do not depend on it (unavailable in this sandbox); paper_fetch.py covers the same ground.
- OCRmyPDF (ocrmypdf/OCRmyPDF). Search-confirmed. Adds a searchable OCR text layer to scanned PDFs via Tesseract. Direct relevance: the two papers we could not read (Hamilton 1989, Bai-Perron 1998 scans) are exactly its use case; flagged scanned_ocr_needed by our toolkit. Not runnable here (needs Tesseract binary), but it is the standing recommendation whenever a machine with install rights is available; re-reading those two scans would close the last access-honesty gaps in wave 2.

## 2. Statistical stack (regime machinery, volatility)

Already planned in the quant-resources application as wave-2 tools; now search-confirmed with repos:

- ruptures (deepcharles/ruptures). Search-confirmed. Offline change point detection in Python: PELT, Binseg, BottomUp, KernelCPD, dynamic programming; exact and approximate. Reference port value: the Bai-Perron methods we adopted prospectively (UDmax, sequential, trimming) are partially implemented here; porting the UDmax criterion and the sequential BIC procedure into the weekly pipeline is a concrete future task for the parameter-stability audit of rule set T (noted as an amendment topic only).
- hmmlearn (hmmlearn/hmmlearn). Search-confirmed. Hidden Markov Models with a scikit-learn API (Gaussian, GaussianHMM, GMMHMM). Reference port value: the 4-state bull/bull-correction/bear/bear-rally template from Maheu-McCurdy plus Hamilton's filtering equations could be prototyped here on weekly data. Discipline note: any regime-count claim needs the Hansen/Garcia critical-value correction, which hmmlearn does NOT provide; we would implement it ourselves (programme lesson from wave 2).
- arch (bashtage/arch). Search-confirmed. ARCH/GARCH family by Kevin Sheppard (EGARCH, GJR included). Reference port value: the up/down volatility asymmetry layer in v3.4 was grounded in Tsay's GJR/EGARCH via book knowledge; arch is the canonical implementation to cross-check any future asymmetry readout. Platform stack: yes.
- statsmodels. Known and already used in the sandbox where available; listed for completeness as the Markov switching (MarkovRegression) reference implementation, which includes Hamilton's exact filter.

## 3. Backtesting engines (platform stack candidates only)

Nothing here touches the weekly programme: rule set T is frozen, the battery is spent, and stage 7 paper testing runs on our own bookkeeping scripts. These are candidates for the parked order-flow platform, ranked by fit:

- vectorbt (polakowo/vectorbt). Search-confirmed. Vectorized numpy/pandas research engine; thousands of parameter combinations at once. Best fit for the platform's edge-scanner phase (Phase 6 of the ORDERFLOW spec), with the caveat that vectorized event handling must never silently relax our pre-registration discipline: the scanner proposes, it never confirm.
- NautilusTrader. Search-confirmed (2026 comparison articles list it among the maintained engines). Rust-core event-driven engine with nanosecond resolution, live and backtest parity. Best fit for the eventual forward/paper execution layer (Phase 8-9), including tick-level order book replay.
- backtrader. Search-confirmed as effectively unmaintained (community forks exist); the 2026 comparisons recommend the alternatives above. Recorded so we do not adopt a dead engine.
- Lean (QuantConnect). Noted in comparisons; heavier, cloud-oriented; lower priority than vectorbt + NautilusTrader which cover research and execution respectively.

## 4. Research platforms (platform stack candidates)

- OpenBB (OpenBB-finance/OpenBB). Search-confirmed (repo, openbb.co). Open-source investment research platform and data-access workspace. Fit: data plumbing and dashboards for the platform, not signal research; our hypotheses remain pre-registered by hand.
- Qlib (microsoft/qlib). Search-confirmed (microsoft/qlib). AI-oriented quant platform: dataset engineering, model zoo, RL, and the newer RD-Agent automation. Fit: the ML-modeling tier of the platform if supervised layers (Cont-Kukanov-Stoikov OFI features feeding a classifier) are ever added; the DoubleML-style evaluation discipline (section 6) would wrap it. Explicitly NOT a starting point: the ORDERFLOW spec mandates null/sanity baselines before models, and Qlib's workflow culture is optimization-first.

## 5. Order flow / limit order book (platform core)

- pgaref/orderbook. Search-confirmed. Order book simulator with limit and iceberg orders. Reference port value: the matching engine and queue-position logic are the cleanest small implementations to port for the platform's Phase 2 (book reconstruction/simulation on Tier 2 data).
- JoshRiang/limit-order-book-simulator and Kushtylish/limit-order-book (both search-confirmed). Price-time priority matching, queue position, spread/depth/imbalance study. Same use: reference implementations for the matching engine port; smaller and easier to audit than a full engine.
- Note: real LOB research data remains LOBSTER (free samples) / Databento / CME DataMine per the quant-resources application; these repos are simulators and reference engines, not data sources.

## 6. Methods (the wave-4 paper queue, tooling side)

- JIDT (jlizier/jidt). Search-confirmed. Java Information Dynamics Toolkit: transfer entropy (including multivariate Gaussian and Kraskov estimators), active information storage, partial information decomposition. Fit: the transfer-entropy paper in wave 4 of the paper queue; porting the Gaussian-system estimator is feasible in numpy when that paper is studied, rather than depending on Java.
- POT (PythonOT/POT). Search-confirmed. Python Optimal Transport: exact and regularized OT solvers, Wasserstein distances and barycenters. Fit: the optimal-transport/EMD resource category; a candidate for measuring regime-state distance (the 3D volume-flow state space of ORDERFLOW Addendum B) without parametric assumptions.
- DoubleML (DoubleML/doubleml-for-py). Search-confirmed. Double/orthogonalized machine learning for causal parameters (partially linear regression, interactive models). Fit: the DML paper in wave 4; the potential-outcome framing is the right tool if the platform ever needs a causal claim (e.g. does OFI cause price impact beyond confounders) rather than a predictive one.
- nolitsa (manu-mannattil/nolitsa). Search-confirmed. Nonlinear time series analysis in Python, including surrogate data generation. Fit: the Theiler surrogate-data paper (wave 4, also the N1-N2 null-test technique for the Elliott enumeration engine). Reference port value: surrogate algorithms (AAFT, IAAFT-style, random shuffle variants) to port into the weekly pipeline's own tooling; the circular-shift null validated in the Amendment 4/5 power readout is exactly this family.
- pyunicorn (surrogates module). Search-confirmed (PIK docs). A second surrogate-data reference with a different implementation lineage; useful as a cross-check for ported surrogate code.
- stumpy (stumpy-dev/stumpy). Search-confirmed. Matrix profile for motif and anomaly discovery at scale. Fit: pattern-frequency null tests (N1) on price series; matrix profile gives an exact nearest-neighbor motif census without window tuning, which suits pre-registered pattern-frequency comparisons.

## 7. How this catalog is used

Immediate (weekly programme, no installs): ruptures UDmax/BIC port and nolitsa/pyunicorn surrogate ports are the two concrete reference-port tasks; both are amendment-gated (parameter-stability audit; nothing evaluated without a signed dated amendment). OCRmyPDF is the recommendation for the two scanned wave-2 papers on any machine with install rights.

Platform start (parked): the stack shortlist is PyMuPDF or pdfplumber (ingestion), arch + statsmodels + hmmlearn/ruptures (stats), vectorbt (scanner), NautilusTrader (forward execution), pgaref/orderbook-class engines as the reference matching layer, OpenBB for data plumbing. Nothing is adopted until Andrea green-lights the platform and the Tier 2 data decision (CME DataMine vs Databento) is made; the 10-20 h/week cap and the standing weekly programme take precedence.

Discipline: no repository was executed; no package installed; no programme data, rule, default, or frozen hypothesis touched; holdout spent and untouched; paper trading only. Search-confirmed status means existence and purpose verified via web search on 12 Nov 2026, not a code audit.

## 8. Addendum (v1.1, 12 Nov 2026 session 17): first usage session, Andrea directive "use the repositories you deem useful for us"

Policy context: package installation remains forbidden in this environment (bundled numpy/pandas/scipy/statsmodels/sklearn/sympy/matplotlib/networkx only). The repositories were therefore fetched as source tarballs (GitHub codeload + the PyPI sdist for charset-normalizer) and run transiently from /tmp with sys.path, alongside three honest compatibility stubs (documented below). Nothing was installed into the environment; nothing below persists in the pipeline.

Used and validated this session:

- ruptures 1.1.9 (BSD-2), run from source: needed two source-level patches to import here: a faithful pure-Python port of its Cython backtracking module (convert_path_matrix; ported from the .pyx + .c, semantics preserved and cross-checked) and a raising stub for the optional Cython KernelCPD fast path (KernelCPD unusable; Binseg/BottomUp/Dynp/Pelt/Window all pure Python and unaffected). ROLE: independent reference implementation for our own exact Bai-Perron dynamic-programming scan (stage 0f). RESULT: full parity, every m, both series, after the parity check exposed a real off-by-one bug in our first DP draft (fixed pre-publication; the ruptures comparison is what caught it). Its Dynp/segmentation source is now the reference for any future port.
- pdfminer.six 20201018 (BSD-2... MIT), run from source with chardet (from source; needed a _version.py shim) and a raising cryptography stub (only the encrypted-PDF path needs it; our PDFs are unencrypted). ROLE: independent cross-check of our stdlib paper_fetch.py extractor. RESULT: maheu_bb extracted cleanly (58,995 chars), bai_perron_2006_sim produces (cid:NN) garbage exactly as our garbled_encoding flag said (the PDF genuinely lacks usable ToUnicode mappings), and both scanned PDFs yield near-zero text, independently confirming the scanned_ocr_needed flags. Our toolkit's honesty flags are validated by a second implementation. The current master branch was NOT used (hard-requires the compiled cryptography package).
- nolitsa (BSD-2), imports and runs from source with a no-op numba stub (its surrogate routines are pure numpy under the jit decorators). aaft executed correctly on synthetic data. ROLE: the wave-4 Theiler/surrogate study's reference implementation; port target confirmed runnable. Not yet used on programme data (wave 4 not started).
- statsmodels (bundled, not fetched): the Hamilton-filter counterpart (stage 0g) used MarkovRegression, the repository implementation already available. Parameter layout verified on the build before use ([p00, p10, const0, const1, sigma20, sigma21]).

Not usable here, status unchanged: PyMuPDF (C-based), ocrmypdf/tesseract (OCR binary absent; still the standing fix for the two scanned papers on an install-enabled machine), arch, hmmlearn, vectorbt, NautilusTrader, OpenBB, Qlib, POT, DoubleML, stumpy (platform stack, parked).

Programme outputs of this usage session (the first concrete fruit of the catalog): research/stage0f_ba_perron_audit.py + report (descriptive Bai-Perron structural-break scan of the unsealed weekly returns and 12-week momentum, own exact DP with ruptures parity, approximate Bai-Perron 2003 response-surface critical values computed from our text extraction of the paper) and research/stage0g_ms_filter.py + report (descriptive 2-state Hamilton filter on the same window, statsmodels). Both are now permanent deterministic checks in verify_all.sh (14 checks, ALL CHECKS PASS). Descriptive only: no forward returns, holdout untouched, no rule/amendment change.

# Quant Resources Application Map (v1.0)

What this is: the three reference artifacts (ORDERFLOW MASTER PROMPT, Top 1000 starter catalogue, Top 100 SALIENT resource list) applied to our work. Three applications were made: (1) the SALIENT artefact checklist (items 091-100) audited against the live weekly pipeline, (2) both catalogues triaged against the market-studies knowledge base with errors the KB itself catches, (3) the genuinely new material assigned to a prioritized paper queue and to the future platform's procurement plan. Nothing in rule set T changed; no amendment implied by anything here.

## 1. SALIENT checklist audit of the weekly pipeline (items 091-100)

Audited against the pipeline mirror (research/, data/, docs/) on 11 Nov 2026. Results:

- 091 Data dictionary: PARTIAL. Column meanings are implicit in stage0c_canonical.py and the reports (week_end, n_sessions, thin_vol_flag all defined in code), but no standalone dictionary doc. GAP.
- 092 Data provenance manifest: PARTIAL. stage0c_report.json records source (Yahoo v8 chart NQ=F, adjustdisabled), fetched_at_utc, null drops and the 22 bad-highlow repairs; stage0d_source_crosscheck_report.json records the cross-source check. No file hashes. GAP (minor).
- 093 Contract-roll specification: GAP. The programme doc allows last-trade continuous at the weekly horizon and requires the splice to be documented; the Yahoo continuous series' roll dates are not documented anywhere. This is exactly what the standing broker/CME 1999-2000 export item would fix. REMAINS OPEN.
- 094 Session calendar: PRESENT. n_sessions and thin_vol_flag columns handle holiday weeks; the stage 7 fetch-staleness guard keys on fetch date vs Friday close precisely to avoid partial-week booking.
- 095 Order-flow feature specification: PRESENT IN SPIRIT. AVFI/VPVI is consistently labeled an OHLCV-derived proxy (the master prompt's Tier 1 proxy-flow tier), never claimed as true order flow.
- 096 VPVI baseline specification: PRESENT. The v3 reference Pine plus stage1/stage1b replication reports preserve formula, undefined cases and warm-up behaviour.
- 097 Pine/Python parity test suite: STRONG. stage1, stage1b (v32, v34, overlay, PGT) replication reports, the TS mirrors, and verify_all.sh. This is the checklist item we exceed.
- 098 Feature leakage audit: MOSTLY PRESENT. Past-only rolling statistics with explicit shift(1) conventions throughout, parity tests, the amendment discipline, the holdout-opened-once record. A formal per-feature shift/truncation test table does not exist as a single document; the discipline is enforced in code and reports.
- 099 Labeling specification: PRESENT. Forward 1-4 week returns, event definitions frozen in the amendments, outcomes timed in the append-only stage 7 log.
- 100 Chronological split manifest: PRESENT. 1999-2013 in-sample, 2014-2020 validation, 2021+ holdout spent exactly once at stage 6b and never touched again.

Verdict: 7 of 10 present or strong, 3 gaps, all three gaps documentation-grade, none affecting results. Action items (no rule change, docs only, next Python-enabled session): write the standalone data dictionary and provenance manifest (091, 092, with SHA256 of the canonical CSV); the roll spec (093) stays open pending the broker/CME export decision.

## 2. Catalogue triage (104 records against the market-studies KB)

Already covered by our notes (no re-reading, mapped to the note files): Fama efficiency reviews (context in The Quants/Björk notes), Madhavan 2000 survey, Biais-Glosten-Spatt 2005, Glosten-Milgrom 1985, Hasbrouck 1991 (book notes), Stoll 1989, O'Hara 1995 (full notes, new), Harris books, Tsay book, Bouchaud book (paused partial), Lo adaptive markets (The Quants notes), Engle 1982/Bollerslev 1986 GARCH (Tsay/Levy notes), Almgren-Chriss 2000 (Cartea/Johnson notes).

Errors the KB itself catches (confirmed against O'Hara's bibliography and our survey notes; fix before any citation):
- Record 051 "A Theory of Intraday Patterns: Volume and Price Variability" is attributed to Lawrence Harris (1986): it is Admati and Pfleiderer (1988), Review of Financial Studies 1.
- Record 075 "The Supply of Dealer Services in Securities Markets" attributed to Glosten/Milgrom-related (1978): it is Stoll (1978), Journal of Finance 33.
- Record 050 "A Simple Implicit Measure of the Effective Bid-Ask Spread in an Efficient Market" attributed to Glosten and Harris (1988): it is Roll (1984), Journal of Finance 39. Glosten-Harris (1988) is "Estimating the Components of the Bid/Ask Spread".
- Record 054 "Inferring Trade Direction from Intraday Data" attributed to Glosten/Harris: almost certainly Lee and Ready (1991), "Inferring Trade Direction from Intraday Transactions Data", Journal of Finance 46. Verify before citing.
- Record 073 "One Security, Many Markets: Determining the Contributions to Price Discovery" attributed to de Jong (1995): the canonical paper is Hasbrouck (1995), Journal of Finance 50. Verify before citing.
- Record 045 (Mandelbrot long memory, 1971) title/venue flagged by the catalogue itself: treat as a research lead only.
- Records 013/014 and 101/102 are internal duplicates of the same papers; the catalogue's own audit flags (093-104) are correct and were honored.

## 3. The paper queue (new study phase, ordered by programme value, fitted to the 10-20 h cap)

Books are done; papers are the next study phase. Ordered waves so medicine never pays for this:

Wave 1, validation spine (6 short papers, a few evenings total; upgrades stage 6 statistics and the future platform's gauntlet): White (2000) Reality Check; Hansen (2005) SPA; Benjamini-Hochberg (1995) FDR; Bailey and López de Prado (2014) Deflated Sharpe Ratio; Bailey, Borwein, López de Prado, Zhu (2017) PBO; Lo (2002) Statistics of Sharpe Ratios. These formalize what stage 6 already does "max-t/deflated-Sharpe-style" and give the exact machinery for the 26-entry stage 7 evaluation, where the honest trial count is the full H1-H11 plus amendments registry.

Wave 2, regime machinery (feeds H6, H10, and the vol-regime clock; also the future regime layer): Hamilton (1989) Markov switching; Maheu and McCurdy (2000) bull/bear identification; Diebold and Inoue (2001) long memory vs regime switching, directly relevant to the long-memory R/S findings in the Härdle notes and a standing caution for Elliott-style structure claims; Bai-Perron multiple structural change. Tools when implementing: ruptures, hmmlearn (both new to our stack).

Wave 3, order-flow core (the future platform's spine; also the Tier 2 confirmation layer for the weekly track's proxy flow): Cont, Kukanov, Stoikov (2014) OFI, the canonical paper for feature family E; Easley, López de Prado, O'Hara (2012) volume clock and flow toxicity, with the registered caution that VPIN is a contested proxy; Cont and Xu (2025) order-book dynamics framework; Kolm, Turiel, Westray (2023) deep OFI as the comparison class the master prompt requires (complex models must beat simple ones net of costs).

Wave 4, method-cautious background (for Addendum B's state-space ideas and the N1-N4 nulls): Theiler et al (1992) surrogate data, the exact technique the Elliott null tests N1-N2 specify; Schreiber (2000) transfer entropy; Peyré and Cuturi (2020) optimal transport tutorial; WATCH (2021) Wasserstein change-point; Chernozhukov et al (2018) double ML for the causal-discipline layer. Strogatz and Kantz-Schreiber stay as reference books, not queue items.

## 4. Resource-to-programme mapping (Top 100 list)

- Category A (data): the only paid decision anywhere in this material. CME DataMine and Databento are the Tier 2 candidates for true NQ flow when the ORDERFLOW platform starts; nothing to buy now. LOBSTER's free sample files allow a zero-cost book-reconstruction learning exercise whenever we want it. The standing broker/CME 1999-2000 export item remains the weekly track's data fix and is free.
- Category B (macro): FRED/ALFRED/CFTC COT are free; needed only when the macro layer (Addendum A4) starts. ALFRED's vintage data is the correct answer to the release-timestamp leakage rules.
- Categories C, I (search, software): already our stack. Genuinely new: ruptures and hmmlearn (wave 2 implementations).
- Categories D, E, F, G, H (papers): triaged into waves 1-4 above.
- Category J (checklists): audited in section 1.

## 5. Master prompt mapping (no execution, for the record)

The ORDERFLOW platform's KB protocol (KB-1 through KB-7) is already substantially satisfied by the market-studies knowledge base: source registry = the notes files, claim extraction = the durable rules/ratios in each file, contradiction log = the inter-book connections and honest assessments, mechanism-first = the mechanism sections. The platform, if and when Andrea green-lights it, starts from a genuinely populated KB, which is rare and valuable. The phases needing Andrea's decisions before any start: data budget (category A), compute location, and the go/no-go itself. Until then: parked. The weekly programme and the stage 7 cadence are unchanged and take precedence.

# Accelerants inventory v0 (the only levers that shorten time-to-proof)

Status: RECORD UPKEEP, executed 2026-11-17, session 87, as methodology-review
candidate D-5 ("a one-page standing statement of the only levers that shorten
time-to-proof... so that seductive 'improvements'... are refused on sight with
a citation"). This document registers nothing, changes nothing, and restates
only measured facts from signed or shipped records; where a number below is
arithmetic on recorded values, it says so. Draft candidate D-5 was listed in
smh_methodology_review_v0 section 5; this page is its execution in the same
record-upkeep spirit as the family registry (D-2). It belongs to no claim
family and opens no clock.

## 1. The binding constraint, stated once

At the frozen NQ design rate (~2.04 compression entries per year on pre-2021
arithmetic, docs/smh_methodology_review_v0 section 3.1): the first kill-line
gate (10 scored rows) arrives in ~5 years, the first calibration readout
(26 resolved windows) in ~13, the two direction peeks in ~20 and ~29, and the
57-of-104 positive proof in ~51 years on NQ alone (docs/smh_proof_criteria_v0,
timing honesty). No statistical method shortens any of this. Only row accrual
does. The inventory below is therefore a list of STREAMS, not of ideas.

## 2. The levers that exist, and their honest size

LEVER 1 (signed, live): the Amendment 9 macro clocks (Gold, Nikkei, DAX).
Design-window entry counts are recorded in docs/smh_crossmarket_stageA_v0
section 3: NQ 41, GOLD 47, NIKKEI 48, DAX 50, on pre-2021 design windows of
comparable length (the fetches begin circa 2000-2001). Per-market design
cross rates are therefore roughly 2.0-2.5 entries per year. Four clocks at
design rates accrue roughly 9 scored rows per year family-wide, roughly 4-4.5x
the NQ-only rate; that is the source of the review's "up to ~4x" line (the
review's 4x is the conservative reading with all rates at NQ's own 2.04). The
honest limits, restated from the signed record: (a) each market's kill lines
score ONLY its own rows (per-market frozen lines; no market's rows advance
another market's gates); (b) the NQ proof object (57 of 104) is not
accelerated by a single macro row; what accelerates is the FAMILY-level
evidence, and family headlines carry the four-market max-t correction; (c)
forward trigger rates are regime-dependent (the NQ design range 0.7-3.2 per
year) and unknown until rows accrue, so the 4x is a design-rate ceiling, not
a forecast; (d) the first macro booking window has so far booked zero rows
(the Sunday 23:45 Malta task is armed; the record says so).

LEVER 2 (his signature away): the C-W1 BTC weekly arm (Amendment 13
candidate, unsigned). If signed and IF BTC's cross rate resembles the tested
markets, roughly one more stream at roughly 2 entries per year, joining the
crypto WHEN family under max-t, never pooled with NQ or the macro family. The
draft's own occupancy warning is part of this lever's honest size: the same
gate that closed C-W3 may close this arm at Stage 0x, in which case the lever
adds zero rows and that is the gate working.

LEVER 3 (data unlocks, his hands only; these open NEW questions, they do not
shorten any existing clock):
- The L2 full download (docs/l2_setup_runbook.md, research/LOCAL_DEPLOY.md):
  unblocks C-E1 and the C1-L2 / C8 microstructure family. A different claim
  family entirely; zero effect on any WHEN clock's schedule.
- The broker/CME 1999-2000 EOD export (Amendment 10, volume/Hawkes,
  data-blocked): unblocks the Layer-4 volume question. Same status: new
  question, no acceleration of existing proof objects.
- Options term-structure data (Amendment 14 candidate, the D-3 draft):
  unblocks the event-structure unseen-ness readout. Descriptive by
  construction; touches no clock.

## 3. What does NOT count as an accelerant, refused on sight with citations

- RE-TUNING ANY FROZEN CUT: the definition-elasticity audit
  (docs/smh_robustness_v0) measured 11 compression cells; the gap is robust in
  every cell but the level is definition-local, and the frozen definition is
  deliberately NOT the grid optimum - that fact is the anti-tuning evidence
  and a standing selftest anchor. Re-tuning buys nothing and forfeits it.
- WIDENING THE ENTRY REGION: the mid clock (docs/smh_mid_clock_v0) measured
  0.470 [0.349, 0.590] with bands overlapping climatology whole. The salience
  is edge-local; widening the region dilutes the signal toward the mid
  non-result. Cited, refused.
- MORE TRIGGERS: the stress side SUPPRESSES the significant week (0.273, the
  stress-entry first-event clock). Adding trigger types adds noise, not rows
  that count. Cited, refused.
- LOWER KILL-LINE ROW COUNTS: the false-kill rate at the frozen gates is
  ~3-4 percent per gate (the stated price of falsifiability). Lowering n
  trades a faster public test for a higher chance of killing a true machine;
  the trade was pre-declared and frozen. Refused.
- POOLING ACROSS MARKETS OR ASSETS: non-negotiable no (family registry,
  cross-family rule 1); pooling would inflate row counts without
  independent evidence and destroy the family max-t spine. Refused.
- A FINER GRAIN OF THE SAME CLAIM: the daily-grain C-W3 arm closed at the
  occupancy gate (7 entries in 6.3 years) and the daily variant of C-W1 is
  pre-declined in the Amendment 13 candidate. Intraday grain of a WHEN claim
  is a new claim family, not an accelerant. Refused.
- ANY BACKTEST AS EVIDENCE: banned by the spine rules; a backtest adds zero
  forward rows by definition. Refused.

## 4. The multiplicity honesty

Every added stream costs family power (max-t corrections grow with the family)
and every headline is a family headline. Acceleration and evidential value are
not the same quantity: four weak clocks accruing fast prove less than one
clock accruing slowly, and the programme's economics (fast-kill, slow-proof)
already price this correctly. The levers in section 2 are worth pulling
precisely because they add INDEPENDENT streams under the same frozen
discipline, not because they add numbers.

## 5. Standing instruction derived from this page

When any future session, session-log entry or proposal claims to shorten
time-to-proof by any means other than section 2's levers, it is refused by
citation to section 3, on sight, without re-argument. This page exists so
that refusal is one lookup, not a fresh debate. It is updated only by dated
record upkeep; no signature is required to read it, and none is pending on it.

# Amendment 10 (DRAFT, UNSIGNED, UNEVALUATED): Volume / Hawkes Layer-4 Microstructure Study, Contingent on the Broker/CME EOD Export

- **Status:** DRAFT, pending Andrea's decision. Never evaluated. No volume or
  order-flow data has been fetched or computed on at draft time; no volume or
  Hawkes number exists anywhere in the programme. Nothing in this document
  changes any frozen NQ definition (LOW cut 0.01739 / HIGH cut 0.02697 /
  2.5-sigma bar / 8-week window), rule set (T/V/V4h), kill line, protocol
  cadence, or the forward log.
- **Drafted:** 2026-11-12 (hourly session 55, PROGRESS block 63).
- **Blocking dependency:** the broker/CME 1999-2000 EOD export (a standing
  Andrea item since stage 0c). This amendment PRE-REGISTERS the study so that
  when the data arrives there is a frozen design waiting, not a blank page.
  Per the programme's hard rule, this data-blocked layer is drafted and
  pre-registered now and is NEVER evaluated before Andrea signs below.

## 1. Motivation and scope

The five-layer architecture map (docs/smh_architecture_v0.md, block 61) closes
with one honest sentence: Layer 4's microstructure half is blocked on data,
and the broker export is the only route by which it becomes measurable at
all. This amendment is the pre-registered design for that half, written BEFORE
the data exists, for the same reason every prior design was: the programme's
recurring failure mode is a design written after the numbers are visible.

Scope is strictly additive bookkeeping on the frozen NQ structure:

- The unit of analysis remains the 41 frozen pre-2021 compression entries and
  their person-week hazard machine. No new entries, no re-definition, no
  re-tuning of the clock.
- The Layer-3 verdict is the NULL TO BEAT: the corrected Cox v0.1 result
  (session 54) showed the compression hazard content is DURATION (pooled
  per-row LOYO Brier 0.07406 duration-only vs 0.07607 with covariates - the
  AVFI covariates made it WORSE out of fold). Any volume/Hawkes covariate is
  tested against that duration-only anchor, and the sp_z decision-bar
  convention (a pre-declared relative-improvement bar) is reused verbatim.

## 2. Book grounding (concepts, not evidence)

Grounded in the market-studies knowledge base; these are the priors, flagged
as priors - none of it is a claim about NQ weekly bars:

- Bouchaud et al., *Trades, Quotes and Prices* (studied to §2.1.4 + chapter
  map): Part IV chapters 9-10, Hawkes processes as the canonical model of
  order-flow clustering, and long-range persistence of order flow. The
  empirics in that literature live at intraday grains; the branching-ratio
  concept (how much future activity is caused by past activity) is the
  transferable idea.
- Cartea/Jaimungal/Penalva, *Algorithmic and High-Frequency Trading*: Hawkes
  intensity as a jump-Clustering model in the execution toolkit; again
  intraday in origin.
- Bouchaud's signature-plot diagnostic (the volatility signature plot note
  in the market-studies base): the flat signature plot of E-mini-class
  assets justifies sampling at the weekly grain as the clean part of the
  curve, and warns that microstructure noise masquerades as mean reversion
  at short lags - the exact reason the volume tests below stay at weekly
  aggregation and treat daily-grain quantities only as week-end summaries.
- Harris (2002) / Hasbrouck (2004): volume is not information by itself;
  the trade-sign and liquidity interpretation requires data (ticks, quotes)
  that EOD bars will never carry. This limitation is accepted up front, not
  discovered later.

## 3. Data requirement (the export specification Andrea can act on)

Required from the broker/CME export, per the standing stage 0c provenance
discipline:

- EOD OHLC and **VOLUME** for the continuous NQ contract, ideally from 1999
  (which also closes the 1999-2000 gap, a separate standing item), minimum
  requirement: full overlap with the frozen series (2000-2020 unsealed
  window) with volume.
- Open interest if available: recorded as context, not as a covariate (no
  frozen definition for it exists; inventing one now would be forking).
- Provenance manifest entry (vendor, download date, contract/roll
  convention) required before any computation, exactly as the canonical
  close series was processed in stage 0c/0d.
- Failure discipline: if the export never arrives, or arrives without
  usable volume, this amendment's arm is CLOSED UNTESTABLE and recorded - no
  substitution of free Yahoo volume (a different construction, different
  vendor conventions, and the stage 0d caveat taught that same-vendor
  cross-checks are weak anyway). A dead arm is a finding, not a reason to
  improvise.

## 4. Frozen constructions (fixed at signature, no post-hoc variants)

All constructions are computed from the export and joined to the frozen
series by week; the frozen clock, entries and person-week table are used
AS-IS:

- **C1 Volume clock (Bouchaud's clock idea, week-end summary):** weekly
  aggregate volume V_w and log-volume change dV_w = log(V_w / V_{w-1}). The
  design question: does compression co-occur with a volume-clock state
  (drying volume), and is the compression-entry hazard conditioned by it?
- **C2 Activity clustering proxy (the EOD stand-in for Hawkes):** a true
  Hawkes intensity fit requires intraday event times and is IMPOSSIBLE on
  EOD data - stated here so nobody later pretends otherwise. The EOD-legal
  proxy is a weekly branching-style ratio: the count of high-activity days
  (daily |return| above that week's own median absolute daily return)
  divided by total trading days in the week, summarized as the week's
  activity-clustering score. It is a dispersion-of-activity proxy, reported
  with the explicit caveat that it is NOT a Hawkes fit and cannot inherit
  the intraday literature's evidence.
- **C3 Entry-condition coverage:** the fraction of the 41 frozen entries
  whose entry week and preceding 8 weeks carry valid export volume. This
  gate is computed FIRST and reported before any test.

## 5. Pre-committed tests (all cells reported, none selected)

Run only on pre-2021 unsealed data, design bookkeeping, mirroring the sp_z
study protocol (docs/smh_spz_covariate_v0.md):

- **T0 coverage gate (pre-declared):** if C3 coverage of the 41 entries is
  below 10 usable entries, the arm closes UNTESTABLE-BY-COVERAGE (the sp_z
  precedent, the H7 occupancy precedent). Counts only, no test.
- **T1 split (counts, wide bands):** entries split at the pre-2021 median
  of the entry-week volume-clock state (dV_w negative vs non-negative):
  entry counts, first-event counts, KM cum12 with bands on each half,
  reported side by side. No selection, no reading beyond the bands.
- **T2 decision test (the only gate that can register a finding):** cloglog
  leave-one-episode-out person-week Brier, duration-only vs duration +
  volume-clock covariate (one covariate: entry-week dV_w), on the covered
  subset. Decision bar, pre-declared verbatim from the sp_z convention:
  the covariate must improve LOYO Brier by MORE THAN 2.0 PERCENT relative to
  duration-only to register as anything; otherwise the verdict is ADDS
  NOTHING and the cell closes.
- **T3 clustering proxy (descriptive only):** the C2 activity-clustering
  score reported as a design table on compression vs climatology weeks;
  explicitly NOT a decision test (two covariates in one model would spend
  multiplicity the design did not budget). Registered at most as a flagged
  seductive cell if a raw split looks extreme, into the CLOSED register
  format (era-confounded until forward-scored, never trusted).

## 6. Window discipline

- Pre-2021 unsealed data only, for design bookkeeping, after signature.
- The NQ 2021+ holdout is PERMANENTLY SPENT. New volume data changes
  nothing about that: no volume/Hawkes statistic is ever computed on 2021+
  NQ weeks, not even descriptively, under this amendment. Export rows
  falling in 2021+ are recorded as availability counts only (the stage0v
  precedent).
- Forward protocol untouched: nothing here feeds T/V/V4h, the kill lines,
  the smh_forecast cadence, the Sunday stage 7 log, or the V4h nightly.

## 7. Honest priors

- The honest expectation is NULL, twice over: Layer 3 closed every AVFI
  covariate cell null, and the covariate that would matter here
  (volume/Hawkes) has book support only at intraday grains on other
  assets. The weekly transfer is speculation until measured.
- Even a passing T2 is design bookkeeping on one market's unsealed window;
  it can never prove the mechanism (the same sentence as Amendment 9's).
  Forward rows remain the only test that matters.
- The study's realistic best case is that the volume layer joins the
  measured tables with an honest verdict, closing the last data-blocked
  cell of Layer 4's microstructure half; its realistic base case is
  UNTESTABLE-BY-COVERAGE if the export is thin.

## 8. Execution gating

- Nothing in this amendment runs before a signature is recorded below.
- On signature, execution order is fixed: T0 coverage gate, then T1/T2/T3 in
  one pre-registered pass, all cells reported, then a dated PROGRESS block
  and session-log entry. No re-runs, no variant grids beyond the splits
  defined above (a grid, if ever added later, reports every cell and never
  selects).
- Tooling (a stage script + verify_all check) is authored only at
  execution time, so the code cannot drift from a signed design.

## 9. Sign-off (PENDING)

- Andrea's decision requested: (1) sign as drafted; (2) amend the
  constructions, the decision bar, or the data specification via a revised
  dated draft BEFORE sign-off; or (3) shelve (the arm then closes
  UNTESTABLE-BY-DISCIPLINE, which is an acceptable and recorded outcome).
- Signed: ____ (date: ____ / decision: ____)

# Amendment 11 (draft): extremity structure of the AVFI weekly series

Change control: sections 4 and 5 of programme v1.0 remain frozen. This
amendment registers new draft cells only, BEFORE any evaluation
(pre-registration), mirroring the Amendment 3 format. It exists so the
extremity readouts exported by strategies/avfi_research_v4_draft.pine
(data-window only) are explicit hypotheses with a pre-committed grid,
per the standing rule: visual configurations become explicit
hypotheses or nothing.

Provenance correction, recorded: the authorization message stated the
Python research "just proved" a "C7 Primed" state is "a statistically
significant predictor of major moves". That statement is false. No C7
or primed concept exists anywhere in the programme record (grep over
the full pipeline tree: only a SHA256 hex fragment and an audit id
substring match). The primed state shipped in the v4 dashboard is
Andrea's draft definition, grain-translated to the weekly universe
(see docs/avfi_pine_v4_hardening_notes.md section 6), labeled
DRAFT-UNEVALUATED everywhere, and is NOT part of this amendment's
registered cells. Nothing in this amendment is proven, significant, or
implied to be either.

## Motivation

The AVFI spread extremities are the programme's oldest unregistered
observation: rule set B, the H8/H9 flags, and the PGT layer all key on
price extremes with flow context, but none registers what the spread's
OWN extremities condition. The v4 dashboard exports prior-bar
percentile ranks and fixed-threshold flags for these cells; this
document is the grid that would govern any eventual evaluation. The
engine evaluation is deferred until the data side is ready (per the
authorization: the Python engine does this when the L2 data arrives);
nothing below has been run.

## Family relationship and honest prior

This family is the extremity-conditioned continuation of a register
that is entirely null so far. That prior is registered here so no
future readout is surprise-framed:

- H8 divTop (n=32): primary 4-week difference +0.17 sigma, p 0.79,
  FAIL, wrong-signed (fade-at-new-high weeks were followed by slightly
  STRONGER 4-week returns than other new-high weeks); unstable across
  halves.
- H9 climaxBot (n=3): 4-week -0.53 sigma wrong-signed, p 0.65, FAIL;
  climaxTop (n=3) p 0.082. Family of two: no support surviving max-t.
- sp_z as a Cox covariate: closed NULL (session 54): coverage 10 of 41
  entries, decision test 1.80% < 2.0% bar, ADDS NOTHING; corrected Cox
  LOYO Brier showed covariates WORSE out of fold by 2.71%.
- H10/H11a power precedent: at plausible weekly effects these
  event-study designs have power 7-22%; minimum detectable effect at
  80% power is around 1.25 weekly sigma for the small-n flags.

Reading: extremity cells at the weekly grain will be sparse (fixed
thresholds on fat-tailed ratios), the tested neighborhood is null,
and the plausible-effect power is low. The honest expectation is that
most or all of these cells die at the occupancy gate or the power
readout. The grid exists so that if one survives, it survives
pre-commitment, not post-hoc selection.

## Precise claims (pre-committed, none evaluated)

Two claim families, stated as conditional structure at the weekly
grain, NQ unsealed data (1999-2020) only:

E1 (effort-extremity vol claim): weeks where the flow engine shows an
extremity of disagreement (|sp_z| or |sp_rz| at or beyond the fixed
thresholds) are followed by ELEVATED forward 4-week realized
volatility (r_var of the next 4 weekly log returns) versus the
unconditional matched-sample distribution. Direction-agnostic: the
claim is about magnitude, not sign.

E2 (asymmetry-direction claim): weeks where the down/up vol asymmetry
is in the high tail (asym >= 2) are followed by 4-week log returns
WEAKER than matched controls; weeks in the low tail (asym <= 0.5) by
returns STRONGER than matched controls. One-sided, two arms, pooled
only under a signed convention fixed in advance.

E3 (extremity percentile continuation claim, registered as the null
skeleton): the prior-bar percentile ranks (extPctSpz, extPctRz,
extPctMom, extPctVol) carry NO conditional information about forward
1/4/12-week returns beyond what the levels already show. This is the
family's stated null; any cell claiming otherwise must beat it under
the grid below.

Claims explicitly NOT made: no top/bottom reversal claim (H8/H9 own
that territory and died there), no curvature reversal claim (the
spread is near-integrated, curvature is noise-dominated at the weekly
grain, and the dashboard says so), no timing claim, no sizing claim.

## Frozen mathematical definitions (as shipped in the Pine exports)

- extPctSpz = pctRank(|sp_z|, 500, 100): share of prior valid values
  (current bar excluded, loop over src[1..win]) strictly below the
  current |sp_z|, na until >= 100 valid prior bars. Same definition
  for extPctRz (|sp_rz|), extPctMom (|mom12|), extPctVol (vol8).
- sp_z = (spread - mu_s) / sd_s, normWin = 500, ddof=1 sample stdev
  (registered v3.3.1 parity definition; current-bar inclusive by
  registration, unchanged).
- sp_rz = (spread - med_s) / rsig_s, median and quartiles by
  nearest-rank over the prior-bar 500 window (registered H8/H9
  definition; interpolation REFUSED post-null).
- rho = vol / va (volume surprise vs EWM halflife 65 baseline);
  mom12 = ln(close/close[12]); vol8 = 8-week ddof=1 sample stdev of
  weekly log returns, annualized by sqrt(52); asym = sigmaDn /
  (sigmaUp + EPS) with halflife-30 EWMs of up/down |r|.
- Fixed flag thresholds (constants in source, NOT chart inputs):
  SPZ_EXT 2.0 (spzAbs2, spzHi, spzLo), RZ_EXT 2.0 (rzAbs2), RHO_EXT
  2.0 (rhoBurst), ASYM_HI 2.0, ASYM_LO 0.5.
- Event sets: all unsealed weekly bars with each flag true;
  percentile-rank cells are evaluated as tercile splits of the rank
  (top/bottom tercile vs middle), not as thresholds, so no new
  threshold is invented at evaluation time.

## Required data

- Unsealed: pipeline/data/nq_weekly_canonical.csv (open, high, low,
  close, volume, week_end, n_sessions, thin_vol_flag), 1999-2020.
  Holdout 2021+ is SPENT and never touched; no cross-market window is
  opened by this amendment.
- Reproduction path: the Pine data-window exports (paste cross-check,
  still outstanding) against a pandas re-implementation of the exact
  cells above; stage-2b conventions for the engine pass.

## Observation horizon and occupancy gates

Forward horizons: 1, 4, 12 weekly log returns (matching H8/H9 and the
event-study spine). Occupancy gates, fixed in advance:

- Any fixed-threshold flag with fewer than 20 unsealed events goes to
  the register as UNDERPOWERED WITHOUT EVALUATION (the H9 n=3 lesson:
  no claim is drawn from tiny cells, not even a descriptive one).
- Any percentile-rank tercile with fewer than 100 weeks per arm fails
  occupancy.
- rho-dependent cells (rhoBurst) additionally require the thin_vol_flag
  and roll/expiry artifact exclusions of the v3.3 conventions; proxy
  volume is inadmissible for these cells (weekly NQ has real volume,
  so this binds only if the grid is ever reused elsewhere).

## Null hypotheses and falsification criteria

For E1: H0 = conditional forward 4-week r_var equals the matched
unconditional distribution. Falsified only if the one-sided
bootstrap p < 0.05 AND the effect survives the max-t adjustment
across the full family AND both sample halves agree in sign.

For E2: H0 = conditional 4-week mean equals matched controls.
Falsified per arm, one-sided, same multiplicity and stability
conditions. The two arms are a family of two inside the family of
all Amendment 11 cells; the family-wise max-t is computed across
every cell evaluated in the same pass.

For E3 (the skeleton null): any single percentile-rank tercile
beating its middle tercile with p < 0.05 is a finding ONLY after the
same multiplicity adjustment; otherwise E3 stands and the ranks are
declared descriptive.

Kill criteria: an evaluated cell that is wrong-signed, unstable
across halves, or under-powered is DEAD and closed like H8/H9; it is
never re-parameterized (no threshold moves, no window changes, no
rank-convention changes after evaluation). Multiplicity: the
Amendment 11 family is E1 (2 cells) + E2 (2 arms) + E3 (4 rank cells)
= 8 cells; a family-wise max-t across all 8, mirroring the battery
protocol.

## Validation methodology (pre-committed)

Stage-2b-validated two-sample event-study machinery: stationary block
bootstrap, B = 2000, mean block 13 weeks, seed 42, one-sided, exactly
as executed for H8/H9. Persistence-preserving nulls per lessons 11
and 13: control populations are matched on the volatility regime
(vol8 tercile) and the trend state (T long/flat), so an extremity
cell cannot win by re-discovering the vol level or the trend.
Forward statistics are computed only inside the engine; the forward
log remains the proof object for anything live, and it is empty.

## Power readout REQUIRED before sign-off

Per the Amendment 4/5 precedent: after the engine implementation and
BEFORE the engine pass, a power readout (power at assumed true
effects of 0.1/0.25/0.5/1.0 weekly sigma at the achieved occupancy,
minimum detectable effect at 80% power) is produced and presented to
Andrea. Andrea may revise or withdraw this amendment after seeing
the power numbers but before any data pass. Given the H10/H11a
precedent (7-22% power at plausible effects, ~1.25 sigma MDE for
sparse flags), the expected honest readout is that E1 and E2 are
under-powered at their natural occupancies; that expectation is
registered now so the readout cannot be framed as a surprise either
way.

## Standing status

- Dashboard: built (avfi_research_v4_draft.pine), observation only.
- Extremity exports: shipped, data-window only, never evaluated.
- This grid: DRAFTED, evaluation GATED on (a) Andrea's explicit dated
  go after the power readout, and (b) the data side being ready.
- Strategy tester: NOT run, per the authorization and the house rule.
- The primed draft state: NOT part of this amendment; if Andrea wants
  it registered, it needs its own grid with the same skeleton.

# Broader panel (DRAFT, UNSIGNED, UNEVALUATED): same clock recipe, future amendment

Status: DRAFT ONLY. NUMBERING NOTE (2026-11-14, session 71): this text was drafted as "Amendment 12" per Andrea's session-70 answer; on 2026-11-14 his directive assigned the 12 slot to the Crypto Accelerator (docs/amendment12_crypto_accelerator.md). This broader-panel draft remains on file, authorized by Andrea as a draft, for a future amendment number to be assigned if and when he moves it. No content change below. Nothing in this document is permission to compute anything. Per Andrea's instruction in session 70 (2026-11-14), recorded verbatim in the session record: "Also draft a broader panel (e.g., S&P, bonds, FX, crypto) as a future amendment for the same clock recipe." It exists so the next amendment debate starts from a written draft, not from memory. No fetch, no bookkeeping, no evaluation, no sign-off has occurred for any instrument below. Stage 0x occupancy bookkeeping for this panel would require this amendment SIGNED first, or a separate dated signed instruction; Stage A machinery would then rerun per market with the same parity gate.

## 1. Proposed panel and rationale

All under the SAME clock recipe: the frozen NQ pipeline (sanity, repair, thin-volume, W-FRI aggregation), cuts transferred by rank (27.52nd / 58.10th percentile of the market's OWN pre-2021 sig_w distribution), per-market sealed 2021+ holdouts, occupancy gates, family max-t, per-market kill lines from each market's own Stage A.

| Slot | Candidate | Grain | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| US equity | ES=F (S&P 500 E-mini) | weekly | Highest overlap with NQ's regime; family-correlation stress case, not an independent replicate; the honest reading treats NQ/ES as a correlated pair inside the family max-t |
| Rates | ZN=F (10-Year T-Note) | weekly | Different discounting regime; the vol-of-vol and flow layers differ from equity; checks the mechanism against a market where "compression" has a different economic driver |
| FX | DX-Y.NYB (dollar index) | weekly | Quote-driven market, no earnings clock; a genuine regime contrast to equity/rates |
| Crypto | BTC-USD | daily, W-FRI aggregated | See the claims-ledger cross-reference below; the ONLY crypto registration in the ledger is C-W1 and only one registration may ever be evaluated |

## 2. Non-negotiable clauses carried over from Amendment 9 verbatim

1. Each market's 2021+ window is a fresh sealed holdout: no computation on it beyond availability counts until that market's own forward rows.
2. No direction splits anywhere; direction is forward-only with two peeks per market, registered in advance.
3. Design statistics (Stage A) are bookkeeping, never proof; no pooled claim without the family max-t across ALL evaluated markets (this panel would make the family up to eight markets: NQ, GOLD, NIKKEI, DAX, ES, ZN, DX, BTC).
4. Per-market kill lines frozen from that market's own Stage A before its first forward row; NQ lines apply only to NQ.
5. Occupancy gate: at least 10 compression entries in the pre-2021 design window or the arm closes UNTESTED-LOW-OCCUPANCY.

## 3. Crypto-specific caveats (new, this amendment's own text)

1. CLAIMS-LEDGER CROSS-REFERENCE: the ledger's only crypto registration is C-W1 ("BTC weekly compression-episode frequencies will beat unconditional climatology"), untested. Only ONE BTC registration may ever be evaluated; any second BTC registration (e.g. one created under this amendment) closes untested. This amendment must therefore either score C-W1 as-is or explicitly supersede it; it must NOT create a second live BTC registration. RESOLUTION PROPOSED: this amendment, when signed, would evaluate C-W1 under the clock recipe (rank-transfer cuts, sealed 2021+ holdout, own kill lines, forward rows), making C-W1 the BTC claim of record; no new registration is created.
2. 24/7 CALENDAR: crypto trades continuously; W-FRI aggregation imposes an arbitrary Friday close. The amendment should pre-register the aggregation choice (W-FRI for family comparability) and note that a W-SUN or W-UTC-Monday alternative is a definition cell, not a free parameter to tune after seeing outcomes.
3. Structural breaks: BTC's pre-2021 window spans the 2017-2018 boom/bust and March 2020; the amendment should record that no stationarity claim is made and the same era-clustering caveat as NQ applies.

## 4. Open items for the debate (deliberately unresolved)

1. Whether ES=F adds information given its correlation with NQ (arguments both ways: family max-t handles it statistically; but a cheaper design might drop ES and add a genuinely different market, e.g. CL=F crude).
2. Whether ZN=F or ZB=F (ultra-long) is the better rates probe.
3. Whether DX-Y.NYB (index) or a single pair (e.g. EURUSD=X) is the honest FX object; the index is a basket and its compression semantics are muddier.
4. Session cadence: which markets enter the weekly forward-booking rotation first, given the 10-20 h/week cap.
5. Nothing in this draft is a commitment of Andrea's time; the standing queue (cross-market forward-booking tooling for the signed Amendment 9 markets first) takes precedence.
